do we need to use pytest.param and especify ids?

> AGENT

No — pytest auto-generates IDs from the parameter values. `pytest.param(..., id="name")` is only needed when the auto-generated IDs are unreadable (e.g. complex objects). For simple values like strings, bools, and enums, the default IDs are clear enough: ``` test_calculate_in_sync[STATE_ON-1234-SlotCode.UNKNOWN-True] ``` vs with explicit ids: ``` test_calculate_in_sync[active_unknown_matching_last_set] ``` The explicit IDs are more readable, but it's extra boilerplate. For this codebase where the parameter values are self-documenting (STATE_ON, SlotCode.EMPTY, "1234", True/False), the auto-generated IDs should be fine. Want me to strip the `pytest.param` wrappers and just use plain tuples?

its fine

> AGENT

Let me explore the current duplicate handler to understand what needs to change:

> AGENT

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────` The key insight: there are two duplicate detection paths that both disable slots but through different mechanisms. The **sync path** (pre-execute `_check_duplicate_code` → `DuplicateCodeError` → sync manager catches → `_disable_slot` with tracking reset) is the clean one. The **event 15 path** (`_async_handle_duplicate_code` → direct `async_disable_slot`) bypasses the sync manager entirely — no tracking reset, no circuit breaker awareness. The fix: when event 15 fires, instead of handling it directly, set a flag on the provider that the sync manager checks. The sync manager then raises `CodeRejectedError` on the next tick. `─────────────────────────────────────────────────` Here's the design: **Current**: Event 15 → `_async_handle_duplicate_code()` → direct `async_disable_slot()` (bypasses sync manager) **Proposed**: Event 15 → sets `_duplicate_code_slot = code_slot` on the provider → sync manager's `_perform_sync` checks this flag before/after set → raises `DuplicateCodeError` → sync manager handles it through normal `CodeRejectedError` path (disable slot, reset tracker, notification) The `_check_duplicate_code` pre-execute in `BaseLock` already handles the "duplicate detected before sending to lock" case. Event 15 is the "lock rejected it after sending" case. Both should flow through the same error path in the sync manager. The simplest approach: after event 15, push a coordinator update that triggers a dirty flag, and have the provider remember the rejection. On the next sync tick, when `_perform_sync` calls `async_internal_set_usercode`, the provider checks the flag and raises `DuplicateCodeError` instead of retrying. Actually even simpler: just have the event 15 handler call `coordinator.push_update` with the current code (triggering a dirty flag + sync tick), and let `_check_duplicate_code` catch it naturally on the retry. Starting with PIN validation: ## Design Section 1: PIN Validation **Config entry setting:** New boolean `pin_validation` in config entry options, default `False`. Added as a toggle in the options flow UI. **When validation runs:** On every PIN save — config flow (new entry), options flow (edit), and websocket `update_slot_condition` (slot card PIN edit). Validation blocks the save and returns an error if any check fails. **Checks (when enabled):** 1. **Duplicate PINs** — same PIN on multiple slots within the same config entry. Error: "PIN is already used on slot {N}." 2. **Simple PINs** — matches a blocklist: all same digit ("1111"), sequential ascending/descending ("1234", "4321"), common patterns ("0000"). Error: "PIN is too simple." 3. **Format violations** — non-numeric characters, too short/long. The lock-specific min/max isn't known at config time, so just check digits-only and reasonable length (4-10 digits). Error: "PIN must be 4-10 digits." **Where validation lives:** A shared `validate_pin(pin, existing_pins, enabled)` function in a new `validation.py` module. Called from config_flow, options flow, and the websocket handler. **Error surfacing:** Config flow shows the error inline (HA's built-in form error mechanism). Websocket returns an error response that the frontend displays. Does this section look right?

Codes may be out of sync." **No new config option** — this is always-on. If your lock is offline for an hour, you should know. Does this section look right?

Good challenge. Let me think through each: **PIN validation** — No, blueprints can't do this. Validation needs to block the save action in the config flow/websocket handler. Blueprints run as automations after the fact — by the time a blueprint detects a bad PIN, it's already saved and potentially set on the lock. You'd have to undo it reactively, which is messy and leaves a window where the bad PIN is active. **Drift detection alerting** — Actually yes, a blueprint could do this. It could watch the coordinator's `last_updated` attribute or the in-sync binary sensor. If it stays `off` or unavailable for an extended period, fire a notification. But it's less reliable than the coordinator tracking failures internally (the coordinator knows exactly why it failed, a blueprint just sees the symptom). **Config flow lock reset step** — No. This is config flow UI — blueprints can't inject steps into the config flow. So: - **PIN validation** — must be in code - **Drift alerting** — could be a blueprint but better in code (more accurate, always-on, no setup) - **Lock reset** — must be in code Should we keep drift alerting as code (more reliable, no user setup), or move it to a blueprint (lighter touch, user opt-in)?

This is the exact problem our `_last_set_pin` fix (PR #962) solves. The loop: 1. Lock has masked PINs (`****`) → `SlotCode.UNKNOWN` 2. Old code: `UNKNOWN + active = in sync` → but after restart, `_last_set_pin` is `None` → sets PIN 3. Push update comes back as `****` → `UNKNOWN` again → old code assumed in sync → but then coordinator refresh triggers another dirty → sets again → infinite loop The issue was from v0.9.6 (January 2026). Our 2.0.0 release with `_last_set_pin` tracking and the tick-based sync system should fix this — after the initial set on restart, `_last_set_pin` matches the configured PIN, so subsequent UNKNOWN states are treated as in-sync. Replaced batteries, node came back 5. LCM enters an infinite loop setting codes The key log evidence: - Codes alternate between a valid code and `********` - "has missing/unknown slots, performing hard refresh" on every poll - Sets slot 1, then slot 2, then slot 3, then loops back to slot 1 The old `_resolve_pin_if_masked` was resolving `****` to the expected PIN, so `calculate_in_sync` saw them as matching. But the lock kept sending push updates with `****` which triggered coordinator updates, which triggered dirty flags, which triggered re-evaluation... and the old event-driven sync acted on every dirty immediately. With 2.0.0 we have: - Tick-based sync (5-second batching, not reactive) - `_last_set_pin` tracking (UNKNOWN + matching last set = in sync, no re-set) - SlotCode.UNKNOWN handled properly This should be fixed. But Michael's specific complaint might also be about the **battery drain from excessive Z-Wave traffic** — even one unnecessary set per restart per masked slot could be concerning for battery-powered locks. > DEVELOPER

can the same slot accept pin and rfid?

> AGENT

In Matter's model, no — a credential slot holds one credential of one type. But a **user** can have multiple credentials across different types (e.g. user 1 has PIN at credential index 1 AND RFID at credential index 2). The credential index is per-type, not global. In LCM's model, a "slot" maps to a credential index. If the user sets a PIN on slot 1, that's credential type=PIN, index=1. An RFID at index 1 would be a separate credential. So the current filtering is correct — when a LockOperation event fires with a PIN credential at index 2, that maps to LCM slot 2. An RFID at index 2 is a different credential that LCM doesn't manage. The only question is: should we eventually support managing RFID/fingerprint credentials as "codes"? That's the broader "get away from PINs" direction you mentioned. But for now, PIN-only events is the right call since LCM only manages PIN credentials.

> DEVELOPER

eventually maybe but I am not sure how that will work cross provider

> AGENT

Right — Z-Wave's user code slots are PIN-only. Matter separates credential types. Schlage is PIN-only. Making it cross-provider would need a common abstraction over fundamentally different credential models. That's a future architecture problem. For now, PIN-only is correct and consistent across all providers. The PR is ready.

Here is my review summary. --- ## Review: PR #985 -- Matter LockOperation event support **Files reviewed:** - `/Users/raman/projects/lock_code_manager/custom_components/lock_code_manager/providers/matter.py` - `/Users/raman/projects/lock_code_manager/tests/providers/test_matter.py` - `/Users/raman/projects/lock_code_manager/README.md` - `/Users/raman/projects/lock_code_manager/requirements_dev.txt` --- ### Critical (90-100) **1. `supports_push` not overridden -- event subscription is dead code (Confidence: 98)** File: `/Users/raman/projects/lock_code_manager/custom_components/lock_code_manager/providers/matter.py` `MatterLock` implements `setup_push_subscription()` and `teardown_push_subscription()`, but never overrides `supports_push` (which defaults to `False` in `BaseLock`). The base class only calls `setup_push_subscription()` / `teardown_push_subscription()` when `supports_push` is `True` (see `_base.py` lines 522, 562, 600, 630). As a result, the event subscription is never activated at runtime -- LockOperation events will never be received. Fix: Add the property override to `MatterLock`: ```python @property def supports_push(self) -> bool: """Return whether this lock supports push-based updates.""" return True ``` Note: enabling `supports_push` also disables periodic polling and relies on `push_update()` calls for value updates. Since Matter PINs are write-only and `_on_lock_operation` only fires code slot events (it does not call `coordinator.push_update()`), you likely want event subscriptions without disabling polling. This may require a different mechanism -- perhaps subscribing in `async_setup` and unsubscribing in `unload` rather than using the push subscription framework, or adding a separate `supports_code_slot_event_subscription` concept. The current push framework conflates "push value updates" with "event subscriptions," and this PR needs to address that design gap. --- ### Important (80-89) **2. `setup_push_subscription` is not idempotent -- violates base class contract (Confidence: 92)** File: `/Users/raman/projects/lock_code_manager/custom_components/lock_code_manager/providers/matter.py`, line 167 The base class docstring explicitly states: "Implementations MUST be idempotent (no-op if already subscribed)." The Z-Wave provider guards with `if self._value_update_unsub is not None: return`. The Matter implementation unconditionally calls `client.subscribe_events()`, overwriting `_event_unsub` without first unsubscribing, which leaks the previous subscription callback. Fix: Add an idempotency guard at the top of `setup_push_subscription`: ```python …

So it doesn't help with "push updates" in the traditional sense (like Z-Wave where we read back the code). The README table entry "Push Updates: No" is still accurate in the sense that we can't get *value* updates pushed — only *change notifications*. Want to subscribe to LockUserChange as well? It would be straightforward to add alongside the existing LockOperation subscription (same `subscribe_events` call, just filter for event ID 4 instead of 2). Good question. Let me check what Z-Wave actually does with code slot events when PINs are masked.

> AGENT

Yes — the Z-Wave code slot events are entirely independent of whether the PIN is masked. Here's what happens: **Z-Wave fires AccessControl notification events** when someone uses the keypad. The event data includes: - `userId` (the slot number) - The notification type (lock/unlock/jammed/etc.) - `event_label` (human-readable text like "Manual Lock Operation") None of this depends on being able to *read* the PIN. The lock just says "slot 3 was used to unlock." So masked-PIN Z-Wave locks still get full code slot event support — you know *which slot* was used and *what happened*, even though you can't see the PIN itself. **Matter LockOperation events are analogous.** They give you: - `credentialIndex` (slot number) - `credentialType` (PIN, RFID, etc.) - `lockOperationType` (lock/unlock/etc.) So the value proposition is identical to masked Z-Wave: **you know which slot was used, not what the PIN is.** The LockUserChange event (event ID 4) would add awareness of *credential modifications* — someone added/cleared/changed a credential. But since we can't read the PIN anyway, the only actionable response would be triggering an immediate poll to detect whether a slot went from occupied to empty (or vice versa), rather than waiting 5 minutes. **Bottom line:** The README "Push Updates: No" is correct — we can't get *value* pushes. The "Code Events: Yes" (which we just shipped) is the useful part, and it works the same regardless of PIN masking.

The sync manager then sees `UNKNOWN` as the actual value, and uses `_last_set_pin` to decide if it's in sync. **So for Matter, a LockUserChange subscription would give us the equivalent of Z-Wave's `userIdStatus` update** — we'd know a slot changed from occupied to empty or vice versa, and could push `SlotCode.UNKNOWN` or `SlotCode.EMPTY` to the coordinator immediately instead of waiting for the 5-minute poll. That's actually meaningful: - **Slot cleared externally** — push `SlotCode.EMPTY` immediately, sync manager detects mismatch and re-sets the PIN within one tick (5 seconds) instead of 5 minutes - **Slot occupied externally** — push `SlotCode.UNKNOWN`, sync detects it needs attention So Matter *could* support push updates in the same limited way masked Z-Wave locks do. The README "Push Updates: No" would become "Yes" with the same caveat as Z-Wave masked locks — values are unknown, but occupancy state is pushed. `★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────` The `supports_push` issue is subtle. In `BaseLock.async_internal_set_usercode` (line 775), after the set call returns, it does `if changed and self.coordinator and not self.supports_push: await self.coordinator.async_request_refresh()`. so now, slot active goes to on -> in sync goes to off -> sync manager sets pin -> sync manager pushes optimistic update to coordinator -> in sysnc goes on -> event comes in with an unknown code for this slot. What do we do with this event? It doesn't seem like it closes the loop on anything

> AGENT

You're right — for LCM-initiated operations, the LockUserChange event is completely redundant. The loop is already closed: 1. `async_set_usercode` succeeds → optimistic push `UNKNOWN` → in-sync goes on 2. LockUserChange arrives → pushes `UNKNOWN` again → coordinator deduplicates → no-op The event doesn't confirm anything the optimistic push didn't already handle. We said earlier it could serve as "confirmation the operation worked," but the optimistic push already assumed success based on the service call not raising. The LockUserChange event just agrees. **Where LockUserChange actually adds value:** - **External changes** — someone adds/clears a PIN at the keypad (`operationSource=3`) or via another controller. No optimistic push exists for those, so the event is the only way to detect the change immediately instead of waiting 5 minutes. - **External clears** — someone clears a slot LCM manages. The event pushes `EMPTY`, sync manager detects mismatch, re-sets the PIN within one tick. So the push framework integration (`supports_push=True`) is really justified by external change detection, not by confirming our own operations. The optimistic pushes in set/clear are what close the loop for LCM-initiated work — the LockUserChange subscription is for detecting changes we *didn't* initiate. Should I update the comments/docstrings to make this distinction clearer?

> DEVELOPER

wondering if we should do optimistic updartes or rely on the events if the provider supports events

> AGENT

Good question. Two approaches: **A) Optimistic push (current)**: Push immediately after service call succeeds. Event arrives later, deduplicates, no-op. Fastest possible sync — no gap between service call and coordinator update. **B) Event-only**: Don't push in set/clear. Wait for LockUserChange event to update coordinator. Slightly slower (event delivery latency) but the event is the lock's actual confirmation that the credential was stored. The argument for B is stronger: 1. **The event IS the confirmation** — the service call succeeding only means HA sent the command. The LockUserChange event means the lock actually processed it. If the lock silently fails to store the credential, B would correctly not update the coordinator. 2. **Single source of truth** — all coordinator updates come from events, whether LCM-initiated or external. Simpler mental model. 3. **Less code** — remove the push from set/clear, remove the `coordinator.data is not None` guards we just added. 4. **Z-Wave is different** — Z-Wave needs optimistic pushes because the value cache update is asynchronous and can cause sync loops with stale reads. Matter doesn't have this problem since `supports_push=True` already skips the post-operation coordinator refresh. The only downside is a brief window (milliseconds to seconds) where in-sync might flicker off→on after a set, depending on event delivery timing. But with `supports_push=True` skipping the refresh, there's no stale read to cause a sync loop. Want me to remove the optimistic pushes and rely purely on events?

Right. Here's the situation with all three PRs and issue #984: **PR #982 (drift alerting)** — If clearing fails with an exception, the sync manager retries and eventually hits the circuit breaker, creating a `slot_disabled` repair issue. This gives the user visible feedback. But if the clear "succeeds" silently (no exception), #982 doesn't help — the optimistic push marks it `EMPTY`, in-sync goes on, and nobody notices the lock still has the code. However, on the next poll cycle (5 min), `async_get_usercodes` would see the slot is still occupied → push `UNKNOWN` → sync manager detects mismatch → tries to clear again → loop. That loop would eventually hit the circuit breaker, which #982 surfaces as a repair issue. So #982 **does** help even for the silent-success case, just with a delay. **PR #983 (lock reset)** — Directly relevant. If the user's Aqara U200 has codes that LCM can't clear, the lock reset step during setup would detect unmanaged codes and offer to clear or adopt them. This gives the user a path to fix the initial state. **PR #986 (already merged)** — The LockUserChange subscription would tell us definitively whether clears are working. If we call `clear_lock_credential` and no LockUserChange event arrives, the lock didn't process it. This is exactly the "event as confirmation" value we discussed. All three PRs together paint a much better debugging and recovery picture for this user's scenario. Should we prioritize merging #982 and #983?

A helper method on the flow class: ```python async def _clear_slots( self, slots_to_clear: dict[str, list[int]] ) -> None: """Clear specified slots on each lock, logging failures.""" for lock_entity_id, slots in slots_to_clear.items(): lock_instance = self._lock_instances.get(lock_entity_id) if not lock_instance: _LOGGER.warning( "No lock instance for %s; cannot clear codes", lock_entity_id, ) continue for slot in slots: try: await lock_instance.async_internal_clear_usercode( slot, source="direct" ) except Exception: # noqa: BLE001 _LOGGER.warning( "Failed to clear slot %s on %s", slot, lock_entity_id, exc_info=True, ) ``` Then the callers become: ```python # Clear step: clear all unmanaged slots await self._clear_slots({ lock_id: list(codes) for lock_id, codes in self._unmanaged_codes.items() }) # Adopt step: clear only masked slots await self._clear_slots({ lock_id: [s for s, c in codes.items() if c is SlotCode.UNKNOWN] for lock_id, codes in self._unmanaged_codes.items() }) ``` Want me to do both in the same PR or separate?

That's a Thread commissioning error — the lock is a Thread-based Matter device (most Matter locks are). The error means HA's Matter integration is trying to push Thread network credentials to the lock, but the lock can't find a matching Thread network to join. A few things to check: **Do you have a Thread border router?** Matter over Thread requires a border router. Common options: - Apple HomePod Mini / Apple TV 4K (if you have one on the network) - Google Nest Hub (2nd gen) / Nest Wifi Pro - Home Assistant SkyConnect or Yellow with the Thread firmware - Any OpenThread Border Router Check: **Settings → Integrations → Thread** — do you see any border routers listed? **If you have a border router:** The lock might be too far from the border router during commissioning. Thread is mesh but the initial join needs to reach a border router directly. Try: 1. Move the lock closer to the border router for initial commissioning 2. Make sure the border router is powered on and active **If you don't have a border router:** You'll need one. The cheapest path is a SkyConnect USB stick ($30) flashed with the Thread/Matter firmware plugged into your HA host. **Alternative: Commission via another controller first** If you have an Apple Home or Google Home setup with Thread, you can commission the lock there first, then share it to HA via multi-admin. But for testing LCM, direct HA commissioning is cleaner. What's your Thread setup look like?

HA can discover them automatically via the Thread integration. **What Thread border router did you think you had set up?** If you go to Settings → Integrations → Thread, what does it show? It might be discovering a border router from another ecosystem (Apple/Google) on your network but not having credentials synced. The "Thread credentials" in the Matter commissioning flow should auto-populate from the Thread integration — if it's asking you to set them manually, it means it can't find a usable Thread network.

There were Matter+Thread integration issues in earlier 2026.x releases that got fixed. `Settings → System → About` should show the version. Can you share the OTBR add-on logs (last ~50 lines)?

> AGENT

OK, I see the actual problem now. The OTBR is forming the network successfully (it became leader), but look at all the `Handle transmit done failed: ChannelAccessFailure` warnings. **The radio can't transmit** — every time it tries to send a packet on channel 15, the radio reports the channel is busy. This is **WiFi interference**. Channel 15 in 2.4GHz IEEE 802.15.4 (Thread/Zigbee) overlaps heavily with WiFi channel 6. The ZBT-2 keeps trying to transmit and the radio detects WiFi traffic on the same frequency, so it backs off and never sends anything. **Why this matters:** The Thread network is "active" (border router thinks it's leader), but it's not actually broadcasting Thread beacons that the lock can hear. So when you try to commission, the lock can't find a Thread network → "credentials don't match any active network around" / "no border router." **The fix: change the Thread channel** You need to pick a channel that doesn't overlap with your WiFi: | Thread/Zigbee | Overlaps with WiFi | |---------------|-------------------| | 11 | 1-2 | | 12-14 | 1-6 | | **15** | **6** ← your current channel | | 16-19 | 6-11 | | 20 | 9-11 | | **25** | minimal | | **26** | none | **Best Thread channels: 25 or 26** (clear of WiFi 1-11). **To change it:** 1. Settings → Integrations → Thread → click your border router 2. Look for "Reset network" or "Form new network" option (in newer HA there's a way to reform with a different channel) 3. Or via the OTBR add-on web UI at the URL it logs (the web UI lets you reform with a chosen channel) **Alternative:** Move the ZBT-2 physically away from your WiFi router/AP — the interference is worst when they're stacked on top of each other. Even …

I actually want to blow my thread network away and start over. How do I do that? I notice the thread key is also the preconfigured one and I'd like to properly set that up

> AGENT

Good call — the dataset you shared earlier has the Network Key field embedded but using HA's auto-generated default. Wiping and reforming gives you a clean network with a fresh key, and it's a good chance to pick a non-WiFi-overlapping channel. **Steps:** 1. **Delete the existing network from HA:** - Settings → Devices & Services → **Thread** - Click your border router (Home Assistant / OTBR) - 3 dots → **Delete** (this removes it from the Thread integration) 2. **Stop and reset the OTBR add-on:** - Settings → Add-ons → **OpenThread Border Router** → Stop - Then: Settings → Add-ons → **OpenThread Border Router** → 3 dots → **Reset to defaults** (or uninstall + reinstall if there's no reset option). This clears the persisted Thread dataset on the add-on side. - Alternatively, if you have SSH access: delete `/data/thread/*.data` files inside the add-on container 3. **Start the OTBR add-on fresh:** - It will boot with no dataset and won't auto-form a network 4. **Form a new network from HA with your chosen settings:** - Settings → Devices & Services → Thread → your border router (it should re-discover) - Click **Form new network** (or similar) - HA will generate a fresh dataset (new network key, PAN ID, channel) **For the channel:** HA's "Form new network" usually picks an automatic channel based on RF scan. If you want to force channel 25 or 26, you may need to do it via the OTBR web UI directly or by manually setting the Thread dataset via the API. **Manual channel selection via API:** You can call the Thread integration's `set_preferred_dataset_tlv` service with a custom dataset, but the easier path is to use the OTBR web UI: - Try opening `http://<ha-ip>:8081` (the OTBR add-on may need port 8081 exposed in its config first) - …

Two important things from these logs: **1. The radio is fundamentally broken right now** The `ChannelAccessFailure` is happening on EVERY single transmit attempt — dozens per minute. This isn't just "channel busy occasionally," this is the radio literally not being able to send anything. Even after the OTBR reformed and became leader, it can't transmit a single packet. A few possibilities: - **Radio firmware mismatch** — the ZBT-2 might be running old/wrong RCP firmware - **Hardware issue** — the radio is failing - **USB power issue** — insufficient power to the stick - **Antenna issue** — but you said it just got migrated, so unlikely physical **2. Settings file invalid + version mismatch** ``` Settings file /data/thread/0_f074bffffe4065e2.data is not a valid OTBR settings file, skipping [NOTE]-AGENT---: Thread version: 1.3.0 ``` Earlier logs showed Thread 1.4.0, now it's 1.3.0. The OTBR add-on version may have changed (or rolled back) and the persisted settings file is incompatible. This means even if the radio worked, the dataset migration would fail. Also: `Running 0.3.0-b067e5ac-dirty` — the "-dirty" suffix means this is a development build, not a release. **Recommended path forward:** 1. **Check ZBT-2 firmware**: - Settings → Hardware → ZBT-2 (or wherever it's listed) - It should show the RCP firmware version - If you can update to the latest Silicon Labs RCP firmware, do it 2. **Verify the OTBR add-on isn't on a beta channel**: - Settings → Add-ons → OpenThread Border Router → Configuration - Check if "Beta" or development version is enabled - If so, switch to stable 3. **The "-dirty" build is suspicious** — that should be a clean release build. Try uninstalling the OTBR add-on completely, then reinstalling from the official add-on store. 4. **Test the radio with Zigbee instead** (sanity check): If you flash the ZBT-2 back to …

Even though the Thread integration in HA can talk to the OTBR via the supervisor network, the **Matter server** is a separate process that needs to discover the border router via mDNS on the LAN. **Things to check:** 1. **Is the OTBR add-on advertising via mDNS?** - Earlier logs said: `Beta mode enabled, using OpenThread built-in mDNS` - Make sure that's still set, OR that the add-on uses the supervisor's mDNS forwarder - Check OTBR add-on Configuration tab — there should be an mDNS-related option 2. **Network mode of the OTBR add-on:** - Settings → Add-ons → OpenThread Border Router → Configuration → Network section - For mDNS to reach the LAN, the add-on needs **host networking** OR the supervisor needs to forward mDNS traffic - Check if there's a "host network" toggle 3. **Restart the Matter add-on/integration:** - Settings → Add-ons → Matter Server → Restart - Then: Settings → Integrations → Matter → 3 dots → Reload - This forces Matter to re-scan for border routers 4. **Verify mDNS visibility:** - If you have a way to scan mDNS from another device on your network (e.g., `dns-sd -B _meshcop._udp` on macOS, or `avahi-browse -a` on Linux), check whether the OTBR is being advertised - You should see something like `<network-name>._meshcop._udp.local` 5. **Check Matter server logs for border router discovery:** ```yaml …

ok I think things are working now, it's just a timeout issue. I will try moving closer to the antenna. It's a Eufy E31 btw

> AGENT

Eufy E31 — good choice for testing. That's one of the more popular Matter locks and supports the User Management cluster we need. Yeah, commissioning timeout is usually a proximity issue. The lock needs to reach the border router during the initial join even though Thread is mesh-routed afterwards. A few feet away is usually enough. A couple things specific to the Eufy E31 once you get past commissioning: 1. **Initial PIN setup** — the Eufy might come with a default admin PIN already configured. After commissioning, check if `get_lock_users` returns any existing credentials. If there are codes set from before, the lock reset step (PR #983) will offer to clear them. 2. **PIN length** — the Eufy E31 supports 4-8 digit PINs. Make sure your test PINs are within that range. 3. **PIN uniqueness** — based on issue #984, Aqara enforces unique PINs across slots. The Eufy may or may not — worth testing by trying to set the same PIN on two slots and seeing if it returns "duplicate". 4. **Event tracking** — once commissioned, try using a PIN at the keypad. The LockOperation event should fire and you should see the event entity update with `pin_used`. Let me know when commissioning succeeds and we'll go through LCM setup.
